Having an IRA can make Backdoor Roth Contributions impossible. Some states slightly favor "educational" retirement payouts, especially public K-12, I think. An IRA looses the connection to employment.

It's a little hazy, but wouldn't most "total withdrawals" rules include a ROLLOVER to an IRA? I certainly had little trouble moving some smaller-balance NFP (via TIAA) and union (401a) plans to an IRA, decades before my retirement.

As I read the thread, you may well be able to get exactly what you want. Is that right? BTW, some of my tiny Not For Profit employers wanted, above all, to have no expenses or effort from their chose of 403(b) provider. So you might emphasize to them (if I'm right about this) that once you get your periodic withdrawals set up, their HR/Payroll/Comptroller office will not need to be involved.
This is a massive university not a small NFP. I am 99.99% certain I can leave the money there forever if I want, subject to RMD rules of course. The money can be withdrawn monthly, quarterly, annually, etc, subject to the rules of the TIAA contracts (of which I hold one, and that one requires distributions over 9 years). If I rolled the non-RC funds into an IRA (which I won't) then my understanding is the 10% penalty would apply.

My question was more how to avoid the 10% penalty, but it looks like that won't be applicable since I will have officially retired. But since the plan documents don't explicity say anything, I need to get add'l information from someone who knows

Giants reportedly interested in Bucs star as trade rumors heat up

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The possibility of the New York Giants pursuing Vita Vea is an interesting storyline for the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, especially if the team keeps sliding down the standings. With the trade deadline approaching, a 3-6 record could force Tampa Bay into difficult conversations about its direction and whether holding on to every veteran piece makes sense. Vea is not just another defensive lineman. He is one of Tampa Bay's most important defensive pieces, and his ability to occupy multiple blockers, collapse the pocket, and create opportunities for the players around him makes him hard to replace. Losing him would undoubtedly leave a significant hole in the middle of the defensive line. ESPN's Jeremy Fowler has linked Vea to the Giants, with New York looking for help along its defensive front after failing to land Jeffery Simmons. FOX Sports reporter Ralph Vacchiano has also said the Giants have a strong interest in Vea and could pursue him aggressively. The Buccaneers, however, are not currently expected to move him.

The Giants' interest is legitimate, too. New York has already made an aggressive push to upgrade its defensive front, including pursuing Jeffery Simmons before ultimately trading him to Chicago. With that opportunity gone, Vea has emerged as another potential target. Reports suggest the Giants could be willing to make a substantial offer to close the deal. But would the Buccaneers actually entertain it?

A deal would make sense from a business perspective. Vea is 31, carries a significant financial commitment, and could command valuable draft compensation from a Giants team trying to make a playoff push. However, Tampa Bay also recently gave him a one-year, $30 million extension, keeping him under contract through 2027. Vea has made it clear he wants to remain a Buccaneer, making a departure especially difficult. The football side of this conversation is also important. Trading Vea would leave the Buccaneers without their most imposing interior presence and could make an already inconsistent defense even more vulnerable. If Tampa Bay still believes it can compete in the NFC South, moving him would be difficult to justify.

This article originally appeared on Bucs Wire: Giants reportedly interested in Bucs star as trade rumors heat up

Odegaard winner spares officials from another VAR row

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

An ultimately routine Arsenal win but, even by the standards of VAR, an utterly bizarre refereeing controversy. Mikel Arteta’s instant simmering fury at being denied a first-half penalty may also have turned into something rather more explosive had Martin Odegaard not later sent the champions six points clear with a ninth win in 10 Premier League games.

The big talking point arose with inadvertent theatre from referee Farai Hallam when Kai Havertz, who was clearly in an offside position, was equally clearly pulled down by Hull City defender Nobel Mendy inside the penalty area. Which comes first? The intervention of VAR suggested the latter. But Hallam duly went to check the pitch-side monitor before then announcing the former.

“After review, there is a holding offence by Blue No 32 on Red No 29,” he declared. Cue cheers from the home Arsenal crown. “However…,” he went on. “Red No 29 is in an offside position, so the final decision will be a free-kick for offside to Hull City”.

#ARSHUL – 42' VAR recommended an on-field review for a potential penalty to Arsenal for a challenge by Mendy on Havertz - but determined that Havertz was in an offside position in the build-up. REF: "After review, there is a holding offence by Blue No.32 on Red No.29. However, Red No.29 is in an offside position, so the final decision will be a free-kick for offside to Hull City".

It all sounded so decisive that most inside the Emirates instinctively thought the outcome reasonable. But then came a closer look at the rules. And Law 11.2 certainly did seem to support Arteta’s protestations: “In situations where a player in an offside position is moving towards the ball with the intention of playing the ball and is fouled before playing or attempting to play the ball, or challenging an opponent for the ball, the foul is penalised as it has occurred before the offside offence.”

Arteta clearly saw no value post-match in aiming much criticism at Hallam following a fourth 1-0 win in 10 league games but did still emphasise his surprise at the decision. “What do you think?” he asked one reporter. “I thought offside, but the law says that doesn’t matter,” said the journalist. “So not offside then,” said Arteta, who stressed that he needs to understand “what is overruling what in the decision-making” after a Premier League statement simply said that Havertz was deemed offside “in the build-up.”

At the very best, it all leaves further confusion and again opens refereeing body Pro Ref to the charge of inconsistency. Fabian Schär, for example, was awarded a penalty for Newcastle against Bournemouth in 2024, despite being in an offside position when his shirt was grabbed by Adam Smith.

Conversely, Dango Ouattara had a penalty overturned for Brentford against Leeds last season after going down under pressure from Gabriel Gudmundsson before being deemed offside. The crucial point seems to be whether the player has actually challenged for the ball at the time of the infringement and, in this case, the answer was surely “no”.

Premier League title triumphs, however, are often forged on forgettable wins in frustrating circumstances and this was ultimately a classic of the genre. With Noni Madueke continuing to step up superbly in the absence of Bukayo Saka, Arsenal’s combination of attacking persistence and defensive resilience was ultimately rewarded by Odegaard’s scrappy 72nd-minute shot. For young players, it was also every bit as educational as the VAR controversy might be for emerging officials.

Madueke had shot low to force a save by Hull goalkeeper Konstantinos Tzolakis, but Odegaard was somehow still first to the rebound, despite being the wrong side of the penalty spot and five yards behind Hull defender John Egan when the ball was struck.

“The way he scores, not the most beautiful, but the most effective,” said Arteta, who said that his team had sometimes tried too hard for a “perfect goal” and should instead be happy with something “more pragmatic”.

It was a well-judged assessment as Arsenal head into the international break with creative deficiencies, but still almost flawless in the cold hard currency of results. Three wins in three days, including at Anfield and in Prague, also underlines a certain champions mentality.

“I need to praise the players,” said Arteta. “It’s been very very demanding; very short time to recover with all the travel. The mentality is incredible. It’s still going to be super difficult. We can improve in a number of areas.”

When it was then put to him that Arsenal have been offside 35 times this season – with Havertz responsible for 15 of those – Arteta acknowledged that “timing” was one element. But, with Havertz targeted by some frustrated fans, he stressed that responsibility was always shared for those split-second moments by the passer and receiver.

He also singled out Madueke, again easily Arsenal’s most creative player, following his surprise omission from Thomas Tuchel’s England squad. “He went away, started to train, he came back and it was a different Noni,” said Arteta, of his reaction to also being out of the Arsenal team earlier in the season. “You could sense that he meant business and he wasn’t going to accept the situation and, since then, I think he’s been incredible.”

An improved showing by Jaissle’s Newcastle, but they will be dissapointed that they failed to see it through after Jacob Murphy scored in the 80th minute.

Arsenal far from their best, but it is another one-goal win in the Premier League. They are now six points clear at the top of the Premier League, and it is 27 points from the first 30 available.

Another save at Craven Cottage, this time from Hornicek to keep out a sweet strike from Sessegnon at the edge of the box. Moments earlier, Dedic brought down Muniz at the edge of the box.

One last change by Arteta: Zubmimendi on for Guimaraes. Madueke could have made the points safe after slick interplay involving Eze and Merino around the edge of the box. Tried to be clever with the finish, the goalkeeper’s save was deflected back towards the goal and it was hacked off the line.

Leno gets down quickly to deny Lewis Hall as Newcastle pour forward in search of the winner.

As good as Murphy’s goal was, Rodrigo Muniz’s finish with the outside of his foot may well be better. Newcastle’s lead was short-lived.

And they have immediately conceded an equaliser, Rodrigo Munoz with a smart close range finish but Newcastle were be mortified with the defending.

Not for the first time Will Osula’s weak hold up play gives the ball back to Fulham but Newcastle had plenty of men back and failed to clear.

Jacob Murphy has scored the goal that some of Newcastle’s earlier good play deserved. Cut inside on to his weaker left foot, and curled a shot beyond Leno from 20 yards.

Newcastle finally have their goal and what a strike it is from Jacob Murphy. A loose pass from Cesar Palacios in midfield gifted possession to the Newcastle winger and he ran forward, cut inside on to his left foot and curled a shot beyond the reach of Leno from 25 yards.

Arsenal’s captain dinks over Tzolakis after Hull’s goalkeeper saved Madueke’s shot. Odeegard was on the move while the defenders were on their heels. Relief at the Emirates, this has been hard work for the champions. Can Hull muster a response?

This is about as dominant as Newcastle have been in a game all season but they have not been able to find a goal from some where. They have just gone close again as Will Osula just fails to reach Amar Dedic’s cross.

Arteta has made another double sub: Hincapie and Eze replace Tzolis and Calafiori, so all change down the left flank. For Hull, Lucas Herrington has replaced the injured Semi Ajayi at the back.

Felt like the cheers for Viktor Gyokeres to replace Kai Havertz were as much a reflection on frustrations with the German as enthusiasm for the Swede. Havertz has struggled today and is far less convincing in this sort of match – when the onus is more on the Arsenal to create – than he was in scoring the winner at Anfield last Sunday.

Specific explanation for Declan Rice’s substitution is unclear – he didn’t look injured – and so perhaps just reflection of risks associated with a third 90 minutes in six days alongside an acknowledgement of quality alternatives on the bench.

Some changes at the Emirates: Cho and Lewie Coyle are replaced by Joe Gelhardt and Brooke Norton-Cuffy.

Arteta with a double sub: Gyokeres and Merino on for Havertz and Rice, which comes as something of a surprise.

Hull’s goalkeeper Tzolakis has just saved with his feet to deny Madueke. A few minutes earlier, Konsa blocked a Cho shot after a threatening Hull break. Still no opening goal.

It has been all Newcastle at the start of the second half and Harvey Barnes has now failed to take a hat-trick of chances. This was the best of the lot, running on to the ball, middle of the area from a perfectly weighted Yoane Wissa pass, he blasted a shot the wrong side of the post.

The second halves at the Emirates and Craven Cottage have kicked off. No changes from Arsenal, Hull, Fulham or Newcastle.

While Kai Havertz was in an offside position when his shirt was pulled, he may not have been active.

The law states: “A player in an offside position is moving towards the ball with the intention of playing the ball and is fouled before playing or attempting to play the ball, or challenging an opponent for the ball, the foul is penalised as it has occurred before the offside offence.”

However, the Premier League match centre does not see it that way.

#ARSHUL – 42' VAR recommended an on-field review for a potential penalty to Arsenal for a challenge by Mendy on Havertz - but determined that Havertz was in an offside position in the build-up. REF: "After review, there is a holding offence by Blue No.32 on Red No.29. However, Red No.29 is in an offside position, so the final decision will be a free-kick for offside to Hull City".

Frustrating first half for Arsenal, but it was never going to be easy to go again in the Premier League following a Champions League away game and last Sunday’s big win against Liverpool. Hull have also played with confidence and resolve.

Hopefully there will be more action in the second 45 minutes at Craven Cottage and the Emirates.

An odd moment at the Emirates. Referee Farai Hallam was sent to monitor to look at a shirt pull on Kai Havertz in the Hull box. Through his headset, he tells the crowd that a “holding offence” has been committed – cue cheers from the Arsenal fans – before adding “however, the Arsenal player was in an offside position”. So no penalty. It would have been very soft in any case, Havertz was not close to reaching the rebound from a long shot.

Mikel Arteta predictably furious to not get a penalty, even though Havertz was clearly offside before being pulled.

I’ve spotted something else since I was covering Arsenal in 2018. I was gazing up at the boards that circle the top tier where the club mark every trophy that the team has won. There was previously this big gap, of course, from 2005 until 2014 before the cabinet gradually began refilling. But someone has since had a good idea. They have added all the women’s trophies as well, transforming the tally by just the odd 17 during those tricky years of transition and billionaire rivals owners in the men’s game.

Declan Rice has also just had a shot flash wide, but still no clear chances for either team.

Dan Burn thought he had scored from close-range, but Wissa has been penalised for blocking Castagne so the goal was ruled out.

#FULNEW – 33' The referee's call of no goal to Newcastle United was checked and confirmed by VAR – with it deemed that Wissa fouled Castagne in the build-up. AR2: "Defensive foul at the back post. Wissa holding at the back post". REF: "Castagne held back by Wissa. It's a deliberate foul". VAR: "He's clearly holding him - confirming the on-field decision of defensive free-kick. Wissa is clearly making an attempt to hold the Fulham defender. Clear holding offence".

Some controversy here as Newcastle appear to have had a perfectly good goal ruled out for an innocuous looking foul from Yoane Wissa on Timothy Costagne inside the box. Dan Burn was the scorer. Newcastle’s bench are furious. Most of us are just bemused.

Arsenal have largely been on top after Hull’s lively start but clear chances are scarce and Hull’s fans certainly sound increasingly confident. “You’re only here for the Tigers”, they say. To which a rendition of “champions” rings out. On that note, they had a replica of the Premier League trophy outside the Emirates before the match, with fans queuing up to get their photo with it.

The bright start from the visitors has not been maintained and Fulham have been on top for the last few minutes. Lukasz Hornicek just made a full-stretch save to deny Oscar Bobb and there is a sense inside the stadium that the first goal is coming.

Newcastle, as we have seen all too often recently, really struggle to pass through the lines and when the ball makes it out wide it invariably seems to go backwards again.

Although Harvey Barnes has just forced a good save from Leno from a Jacob Murphy cross. It was easily Newcastle’s best move of the match as Wissa and Osula linked up for the first time through the middle.

David Raya to Arsenal’s rescue, plucking Stroud’s shot away from the bottom corner after a Calafiori giveaway. This has been a positive opening 20 minutes from Hull.

Arsenal with a penalty shout, turned down.

Kai Havertz was going away from goal but Nobel Medy hardly subtle with his challenge – arm and leg – on the edge of the area. It would have been what we call soft but the sort of innocuous challenge that very often results in a foul elsewhere on the pitch.

On a self-indulgent note, this is an all-too-rare trip to the Emirates for me. I covered Arsenal for the last decade of the Arsene Wenger era, ending in 2018 and haven’t made many visits since. A few things are already striking: so many familiar faces still among the staff at a club that has always had more of a family feel than most. Outside, and few of the gaps in the middle (or top tier) that became evident during the final Wenger years when many fans clearly lost faith in the team’s title-winning credentials. The atmosphere is a notch up too.

Thiaw heads wide after a well-worked short corner from Newcastle, but the flag is up for offside in any case. Lewis Hall with the cross.

A decent start from Newcastle United against Fulham and you can see clearer patterns of play emerging. The fact that Fulham are constantly looking to build their attacks by playing out from the back has also helped. The home side have had a couple of scares already.

Hull gain some territory in the early exchanges, and win a corner via a long throw. From the set-piece, Egan’s set-piece hit the side-netting. A shot across Arsenal’s bows.

The lack of Mohamed Beloloumi’s creative spark clearly a big blow for Hull against the best defence in the league; a defence that will be further strengthened by the imminent return of William Saliba. Mikel Arteta had decided that Saliba would be better served by playing against Aston Villa for the Under-21s at Boreham Wood tomorrow. Hull have started on the front, though, in the opening minutes

Updates to follow from the Emirates and Craven Cottage. The Last Post was played and there was a minute’s silence as both grounds mark Remembrance Day.

It’s always great to be back home. It’s the last game before the international break so we know the importance of that and the challenges Hull will provide today. We’re super focused, it’s a massive game for us.

With the amount of games we’re playing, we need that freshness. The team has been performing really well, the finishers have been key again and that’s what we have today – start the game really, really strong and if we need to, have the ability to change the game.

Obviously, the Champions League allows you to bring more players into the bench, but we believe that it’s the best thing to do: to play some minutes at the weekend with the Under-21s and then be fully fit for the international break.”

Arsenal are playing very good football, no matter Champions League or Premier League. They are very strong, they control everything not just against us, also other better clubs. But in football, we have a chance. Obviously, they are a big favourite, and we can start to try to create some difficulties for them.

The return of Sean Steur is a positive move from Newcastle manager Matthias Jaissle but is it also a sign the German is feeling the pressure of public opinion on Tyneside?

The 18-year-old has looked the best of Newcastle’s summer signings but Newcastle are understandably trying to ease the teenager into things.

Ultimately, though, Steur is the only midfield player who can take possession of the ball under pressure and despite his tender years they have looked a better side with him in it.

There have been widespread calls for Steur to be called back into the side and Jaissle has done just that for a game he could really do with winning coming off the back of four defeats in their last five games.

Malick Thiaw also comes back into the side for Fabian Schar, with Jacob Murphy preferred to Anthony Elanga and Yoane Wissa starting in place of Joe Willock. I don’t think any of those dropped can have any complaints.

However, the constant chopping and changing betrays the problems Jaissle is facing. Whatever team he sends out is struggling to deliver what he wants, both individually and collectively.

With the international break coming up, Newcastle need a positive result to quieten the negative noise.

Mikel Arteta has said Arsenal are “looking after” Declan Rice more now than in any previous campaign and has rejected suggestions that the England midfielder is being overplayed.

Rice played through nerve pain in his hamstring in the second half of last season, when he made an extraordinary 69 appearances for club and country.

The 27-year-old has played at least 60 matches in each of the past four campaigns, prompting concerns among Arsenal supporters that he is at risk of burnout or injury.

Arteta kept Rice on the field for the full 90 minutes of Arsenal’s midweek victory over Slavia Prague but insisted the club is working harder than ever to manage the midfielder’s workload.

So far this season, Rice has been left out of Arsenal’s two Carabao Cup matches and also withdrew from the previous international break in September.

Asked if he was concerned about Rice’s fitness, Arteta said: “Concerns? Which is the only player, the only player in this squad, that for two games in the Carabao Cup has been left out of the squad? At home with two days off? It’s not true [that Rice is being overplayed].

“We are really looking after him more than any other season. The way he is training and all the love [he is being shown]. That’s it.

“Here are the demands of this football club and a player like him. Not only him, because there are many others that have even less time. That’s it. That has to be the mindset. If you start to put other things in your brain, [it will create] damage. Erase them, get them in the bin and just focus on what you have to do.”

Newly-promoted Hull City stunned Arsenal at the Emirates thanks to goals from Geovanni and Daniel Cousin in September 2008, but you will find long odds on them repeating the feat this afternoon.

With Manchester City playing at Nottingham Forest in the later kick-off, Mikel Arteta’s team have the chance to extend their lead at the top of the Premier League and make it 27 points from a possible 30 going into the international break.

Victory at Anfield last weekend was achieved with steel and resolve, but the more intricate side of Arsenal’s attacking play will be under the microscope against opponents likely to defend deep and in numbers.

With that in mind, it was noteworthy that Kai Havertz, Martin Odegaard, Bruno Guimaraes and Myles Lewis-Skelly started the Champions League win at Slavia Prague on the bench, with only Odegaard coming on.

Declan Rice played the full 90 minutes, and one wonders if this is a rare chance for Arteta to leave him out of a Premier League starting XI.

Hull are 12th and have lost only two league games, a performance which far exceeds the gloomy pre-season forecasts for their chances. However, they are winless since the end of August and have not kept a clean sheet since a goalless draw with Aston Villa on Sept 4.

There is one other three o’clock kick-off: Fulham host Newcastle United at Craven Cottage, with Matthias Jaissle under increasing pressure.

Newcastle were booed off at St James’ Park on Monday after their limp defeat by Everton, and have looked far too easy to play through all season, with their defensive statistics making for grim reading.

Alvaro Arbeloa was in Jaissle’s position a few weeks ago, but Fulham appear to have turned a corner and were impressive in a 1-1 at Villa Park last time out. There were reports of dressing room unrest, but Fulham rank highly for possession and chance creation so it appears the Spaniard is putting his stamp on things.

What we know about Krakow mass shooting so far

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

At least six people have been killed and several others injured after a gunman suspected to have ties to the far-right opened fire in central Krakow, the second largest city in Poland. Here's everything we know so far.

The attack began shortly before 15:00 local time (14:00 GMT) in Krakow's Main Market Square, the bustling central point of the city.

Police said a man drove into the square before beginning to fire "at random people from his car".

Footage from the scene shows scenes of panic as people fled, with the sound of gunfire clearly heard.

People run for shelter as gunshots heard in Krakow

Armed officers raced to the scene and engaged in a gunfight with the attacker, who was shot within three minutes of the attack beginning, preventing a further massacre, officials said.

The gunman was shot but is alive and has been arrested.

Distressing footage authenticated by BBC Verify shows the moment a police officer confronts the suspect on the ground before firing his weapon several times.

The officer then falls to the ground, apparently having been injured himself.

Moment police officer appears to shoot Krakow suspect

Shop windows in the square have signs of damage, seemingly from bullets, with glass shards scattered along the floor.

City officials have urged people who live in the city and tourists to stay away from the area while an investigation continues.

Six people - four women and two men - are confirmed to have been killed, including a foreign national.

Sixteen people, including the attacker, were taken to hospital. Among those being treated are two police officers who were shot while detaining the attacker.

One of the police officers was described as being in a "very critical condition" by a Polish government minister after being shot three times while arresting the attacker. The officer is undergoing surgery, he added.

Polish authorities have not confirmed the names and ages of the other victims.

Police have identified the attacker as a 25-year-old Polish man, but have not released any further details about his identity.

The suspect was wearing "military-style attire" and had a military-grade weapon, according to a police spokesperson. However, authorities have confirmed the attacker was not a soldier.

"Many pieces of weapons and ammunition were found in his vehicle", the spokesperson added.

Polish media has reported the suspect had a firearms permit.

A minister has said early investigation indicate he was inspired by "far-right ideologies", without elaborating further.

His home is being searched with support from counter-terrorism police.

The shooting happened in one of the largest medieval squares in Europe, a busy location which is also a popular tourist destination.

Jonathan Balshaw from Ipswich was there with his Polish girlfriend Dominika. The couple were outside St Mary's Basilica, in the corner of the square, when the shooting started.

Jonathan said: "We ran down the side of the church and through the side door and hid with several other people until the gunfire died down.

"A lot of shots were fired, more than 20 with it sounding like multiple different firearms - there were two distinctly different noises."

Caitlin Shasby from Newcastle arrived in Krakow yesterday with her friend Jon for a long weekend.

She said they heard shots from their hotel room but were not sure what was happening until their hotel rang them and told them to "stay in our rooms until further notice".

Polish Prime Minister Donald Tusk is on his way to Krakow and is expected to give an address later on Saturday.

Earlier, he said he was being kept informed by the interior minister and that police were investigating "the background and all the circumstances".

Krakow Mayor Åukasz GibaÅa has signed an order declaring a period of mourning in the city which will last until Wednesday, November 11.

President Karol Nawrocki said he was "deeply concerned" and was awaiting "a full explanation of the circumstances surrounding these tragic events".

US ambassador to Poland Tom Rose described it as a "savage and murderous attack".

The EU's top diplomat, Kaja Kallas, said the shooting was "a tragedy that will be felt far beyond Poland".

Gunman who killed six in Krakow believed to have been inspired by far-right ideology, minister says

’Saturday Night Live’s Famous Formula Has 1 Unfixable Problem

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Greer Riddell is a London-based film and television journalist writing about comedy, streaming and the evolving media industry in the UK and USA. She works at Collider, covering the latest in film, television and entertainment through interviews, features, reviews and industry coverage.

Her recent brand collaborations include HBO, Warner Bros., Netflix, Hulu, Sky, Disney+ and DC Studios. She has covered titles including Saturday Night Live UK, Rivals, Scrubs, Stuart Fails to Save the Universe, Ludwig, Supergirl and Stranger Things.

Her recent interviews include Florence Pugh, Mike Faist and Christopher Abbott for Netflix's East of Eden, Taika Waititi and Jenna Ortega for Klara and the Sun.

Saturday Night Live has spent 5 decades establishing a format that is instantly recognizable and runs like clockwork. Bringing the show to the UK meant deciding which of those traditions were worth keeping and which could be left behind. At the Edinburgh TV Festival 2026, SNL UK director Liz Clare and cast member Emma Sidi discussed two elements of the original that prompted very different reactions — the famous crane shot and the end-of-show credits.

While Clare rejected the crane because of the practical demands of the British production, Sidi admitted that the cast initially found the closing tradition unbearably "naff." SNL UK could not reproduce the American original and expect every production choice or cultural convention to translate, but neither could it reinvent everything without losing elements that make the format successful. So before any episode aired, the crew behind SNL UK had the mammoth task of working out what it could change and what it needed to keep, and while one problem solved itself, another remains unfixable.

One of the defining features of Saturday Night Live is its production setup, which allows the show to move between the monologue and sketches while maintaining the pace and energy of a live performance. SNL UK has adopted much of the American show's approach, but director Clare decided against using its famous crane shot. Speaking at the Edinburgh TV Festival's The SNL Masterclass, director Clare explained, “The camera setup is probably pretty standard. In fact, it's based on the American show, bar the fact that we don't use the Americans have got this big old bit of kit, big crane that's become a feature of the show,” Clare said. “We decided not to employ that camera because it takes up an awful lot of space.”

Britain makes more detective television than any country has any business making, and somehow almost all of it is good. Here is the one you belong in, what it says about you, and the case for it being the best of the four.

You want the tea, the gossip and the vicar behaving oddly. Midsomer is a county where the scenery is beautiful, the fete is on Saturday and the murder rate is roughly that of a small war, and absolutely nobody involved thinks this is strange.

You feel other people's grief as if it were handed to you personally. Broadchurch is less a whodunnit than a study of what a small town does to itself when it finds out that the answer was always going to be somebody everybody already knew.

You go through things rather than around them. John Luther is the best detective in London and a liability to everybody who works with him, and the show is completely honest that those two facts are the same fact.

You say the thing and you do not dress it up. Vera Stanhope turns up in a hat and a mac, is rude to almost everybody, and is the only person in the room prepared to ask the question that the family has been waiting years for somebody to ask.

The decision makes sense for a show that has to accommodate multiple sets, rapid changes and performances staged in the round. A large crane might be a recognizable part of the American production, but that does not mean it is essential to the format. Instead, the British production has adapted its equipment to suit the way the show operates. “But we do have a jib, and we've got some pads,” Clare explained, describing some of the alternatives available to her team. Her approach is less about reproducing the American show's visual identity exactly and more about understanding what its camera work is trying to achieve. As she put it, “It's really just about making sure that what is interesting about the show is that it's played in the round in a way that then helps all of those resets.”

Jamie Dornan’s turn as host in Season 1, Episode 2, offers a good example of how the money saved on the crane allows the series to follow the technical needs of each joke. Dornan played a criminal taking Annabel Marlow hostage, a role that required the production to recreate the appearance of a video recorded on a camcorder. Rather than relying on a conventional studio camera to capture the scene, Clare's team found a more specific solution. “We knew that we needed some kind of camera that he could look like he was recording a sort of hostage video,” she explained. “So we actually got a live really lovely sort of digicam that was a live picture, live feed that we were able to take out of the camera and put in and used to make it look as authentic as possible.”

'SNL UK' Head Writer Jonno Johnson lifts the curtain on the UK’s buzziest comedy.

The closing sequence of Saturday Night Live is one of its most familiar traditions. As the credits roll, the cast gathers around the host, exchanging hugs and saying goodbye to the audience. For Sidi, however, the practice initially seemed awkward and unnecessary. Her reaction highlights a different challenge for SNL UK. Technical decisions can be made according to the needs of the production, but cultural traditions can be harder to assess up front. Sidi admitted that she had never been particularly fond of the American show's endings. “That's something with the American show... the ending, the goodbyes bit. I would just see it and go, ‘This is just so naff. I can't bear to see this,’” she said at the Edinburgh TV Festival. From the viewer's perspective, the sequence can look like an overly sentimental ritual, particularly when performers who have spent the episode confidently delivering jokes suddenly show their relief that it is over in the farewell.

However, making SNL UK changed Sidi's perspective. The cast were experiencing the pressure of putting together a live show, working through rehearsals, changes, and the demands of performing. Sidi explained that she understood the tradition differently once she had experienced that process, “And when we're doing it now, I get it because we have done the heist during the week, you know?... So we're hugging and crying... fuck it, we're luvvies now,” she joked. “You are just like, thank god we did it. It's so real that hugging at the end.”

SNL UK could have dropped the closing sequence to avoid something the cast found embarrassing, just as Clare dropped the crane. Removing them would not change the quality of the series or make a sketch easier to stage. Yet it would have removed a moment that the people making the show have come to value. Sidi even acknowledged that viewers might still find the sequence uncomfortable: “So sorry you have to watch that, but we're gonna keep doing it!” Now halfway through its second season, Saturday Night Live UK is still developing its own identity away from the American original, but some things stay essential to the show regardless of where it is being made.

Saturday Night Live UK Season 2 Part 2 returns on Sky One in the UK in January 2027 and streams the next day on Peacock in the U.S.

Mikel Arteta identifies two missing elements that can raise Arsenal’s level

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Arsenal 1-0 Hull: The Gunners’ manager called for more pragmatism and chaos after Martin Odegaard’s goal secured a familier scoreline for Arsenal

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

After a familiar scoreline, Mikel Arteta has a new phrase.

The Arsenal manager used some variation on “insist” several times throughout his press conference for the win over Hull City, after his team had almost been left standing at the door.

Martin Odegaard instead offered a captain’s goal, for a third 1-0 win in four in the league. Like many of those, it could have been worse, but could have been better – especially in terms of finishing.

Arsenal currently always feel one setback away from a big discussion about which forward they need to sign in January. They are obviously monitoring a few options, and are expected to do some business, but Arteta clearly doesn’t want his players thinking like that.

When asked about what was actually a sixth win by one goal out of 10 Premier League games so far, he said they “need to be more efficient”.

It could be argued that such a return is the height of efficiency, and that Arsenal could have been further ahead had they benefited from that debated penalty decision – but you know what he meant, especially with regard to finishing.

Arteta lamented how his team had two further one-on-ones in the game, only to waste them. He said it was like they “are trying to score the perfect goal”.

That’s another familiar refrain with this club, if from a different era to the 1-0s. Arteta’s desired solution is to be “more pragmatic”, that they “need to be more chaotic”.

That’s undeniably true, but the manager being the one to say this poses some questions of his own.

Another phrase that Arteta used was “volume”. That, when you face a defence as well structured as Hull City, you need to be “generating volume”. In other words, numbers, a critical mass of chances where you eventually score – which is pretty much what they did. Insistence.

It’s been discussed on these pages before that Arteta is a huge “probabilities guy”, where he sees almost every game as an equation. If Arsenal consistently generate an xG of 2 or above and limit the opposition to 0.5, they are likely to win enough games to win the league.

A natural extension of this is an exactingly calibrated team, where Hull City players – like so many others – were left praising “the shape”.

But is that mentality something that encourages “pragmatism” or, as Arteta elaborated, “chaos”? Other descriptions for that are sparkle, ingenuity… magic.

That’s hard to find sufficient space for in a system that demands a lot of energy devoted to pressing and structure.

Odegaard, the match-winner, is almost the ultimate example. The playmaker is capable of some of the most ingenious and exquisite touches in the league, but it does not feel like he is as characterised by that as much any more.

When you ask people at Arsenal about him, in fact, they praise how he understands Arteta’s pressing better than anyone else. This is why he is captain and what they were said to really miss last season when he was out. Odegaard leads the press. When he goes, everyone immediately knows when and where to follow.

An irony is that Noni Madueke is one of the most chaotic players in the league – in a good way. He is a rare attacker that takes people on. As is the case when you see his and Christos Tzolis’s names on the teamsheet, you don’t quite know what you’re going to get in terms of performance level. Club insiders say it could be anything between a two and an eight out of 10.

The most frustrating thing about Madueke, in fact, is that he usually ends such runs with a predictable cross rather than real variation. He doesn’t shoot enough, which touches on Arteta’s point about trying to score “a perfect goal”.

All of this came together for a moment that should have sealed the win.

Arsenal’s attack put together a brilliant flowing move at 1-0, to leave Madueke through for one of those one-on-ones. It was close to perfect. Until, with just Konstantinos Tzolakis to beat, Madueke tried to roll the ball past him only to get his feet wrong.

While the forward clearly has potential, the point of this is that Arsenal still feel at least one attacker – but arguably two – away from really being at the level they want to be.

It should be stressed that their current level isn’t exactly much of a problem. They’ve won nine out of 10 in the league. They’re brilliantly efficient in everything outside the scoring.

Even this match has to be put in the context of a tough match against a very respectable Hull City side. They’re so well structured you can see them being this season’s Sunderland.

Arsenal were known to be impressed afterwards, with Arteta especially respectful. His team still get through, and you can see them being convincingly ahead by Christmas.

But the league isn’t the level anymore. It’s about beating Paris Saint-Germain in the Champions League, about being European champions as well as English champions.

That is something they really have to insist on.

Opinion: Richard Hughes has left Liverpool but his transfer mistakes remain

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Richard Hughes was in charge of Liverpool's transfer business during their most expensive window ever.

Last season, the Englishman spent over £450 million to try and improve the Reds' ranks.

Most people thought this amount of spending would set them up for the next few years but, in reality, that was far from the case.

Alexander Isak and Jeremie Frimpong were constantly injured with Florian Wirtz often failing to live up to expectations.

The same issues followed Liverpool into the 2026-27 season where he spent another £150 million trying to resolve those same issues.

However, now that he's gone, fans are hoping for change but, unfortunately for them, Hughes' mistakes have remained.

Liverpool are still having to suffer with having a trio of veterans among their ranks thanks to Richard Hughes

Although Hughes may have felt like he did a good job as Liverpool's sporting director, it feels like he's actually managed to make the team worse in some ways.

He handed two new long-term deals to Ryan Gravenberch and Dominik Szoboszlai who have both been poor and, he attempted to replace Mohamed Salah with a £123 million deal for Bradley Barcola.

On the face of things, these may have felt like the right things to do at the time, but, now that we're a few months into the 2026-27 season, it's clear that they weren't.

His failure to offload some of Liverpool's benchwarmers is also beginning to hurt the Reds.

Wataru Endo, Kostas Tsimikas and Federico Chiesa are all still at the club despite not having a regular role with the Premier League outfit for some time.

And, while Julian Ward could end up generating a little bit of cash thanks to their sales, both Endo and Tsimikas' contracts expire at the end of the current season which means they're likely to leave for nothing.

But, until then, they're just taking up valuable squad spaces which has left the Reds a bit stuck.

Julian Ward needs to get Liverpool's first-team squad back to its best

Although Liverpool's performances are down to Andoni Iraola, the vast majority of their recruitment falls onto Ward's shoulders.

He was appointed as the Reds' new sporting director after Hughes chose to leave his role and, since then, several players have been linked with a switch.

The Reds may be one of the most well known football teams in the world but, that doesn't mean that they have an endless amount of money to spend.

However, with how much damage Hughes has done to their squad, it's clear that so many areas of it will need to be upgraded.

Without Liverpool doing this, they could get left even further behind their title rivals which is why it's so important for Ward to get the squad back up to scratch.

Here's hoping that Ward and Iraola can solve Liverpool's issues and get them back to where they need to be.

France sees new round of Yellow Vest, student protests

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Although fewer protesters turned out in comparison with previous weeks, several cities saw clashes between police and demonstrators.

Fewer protesters than expected turned out for the fourth weekend of mass demonstrations in France, although clashes with police were reported in several cities, the Interior Ministry said on Saturday.

Authorities had expected up to 170,000 people to protest nationwide, including 60,000 in Paris.

The ministry reported a slightly lower nationwide total of 146,000 people, out of which some 46,000 took to the streets of the French capital, where the afternoon was reported to have remained mostly calm, with some isolated incidents reported in the evening involving the deployment of tear gas.

Protest organizers gave a higher nationwide count of over 250,000 protesters, which is nevertheless fewer than the 320,000 to 500,000 that were reported to have turned out last Saturday.

Clashes between police and protesters were reported in Toulouse, Marseille, Lyon and Bordeaux.

The Interior Ministry reported some 350 arrests nationwide by the evening in connection with the protests.

Students and labor unions had called an authorized central Paris march "against the high cost of living and in support of young people."

In Paris, an authorized march proceeded peacefully from Republique to Nation, but tensions erupted after police said some 200  "violent individuals" attacked law enforcement after a man was arrested for setting fire to a trash can, according to Paris' police headquarters.

Separately, the country's so-called "Yellow Vest" movement — which gained notoriety eight years ago by violently protesting a proposed fuel tax hike rejected invitations to join the student rally.

Instead, the "Gilets Jaunes" held a banned event at Paris' Place de la Concorde, close to parliament and the presidential Elysee Palace. Two of its leading figures, Benjamin Ball and Solene Azelma Sigaux, were arrested and taken into custody.

Ahead of Saturday's protests, French Interior Minister Laurent Nunez said 70,000 police and security forces would be deployed across the country over the weekend, adding that authorities will "be ready" for "rioters and looters."

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

The large police presence comes on the heels of weeks of mass demonstrations that have at times grown extremely violent, most notably on October 17, when France's worst rioting in years resulted in more than a billion euros in damage to private and public property.

On Thursday, police made over 600 arrests during a nationwide strike that included blockades and other protest action.

Authorities say that, for the most part, protests have remained peaceful.

But there have continued to be numerous incidents of violent clashes with police, as outgoing President Emmanuel Macron and his government struggle to address public discontent over the rising cost of living.

Although Prime Minister Sebastien Lecornu is scheduled to meet with trade and employee unions on Monday, organizers say the only thing that will quell anger will be for the government to bow to their demands.

"The only thing that will bring the protests to an end is a response to the demands that have been put forward for several weeks by workers, civil servants, young people and pensioners," according to an inter-union coalition statement published on Friday.

This latest round of protests began with students calling for improvements to the national education system and unions joining in to call for wage hikes and tax reforms.

Macron's government has said it intends to raise the minimum wage as well as increase funding for schools and teaching staff. But it finds itself in a tight fiscal spot as debt — currently 119% of GDP — continues to run out of control in the EU's second-largest economy.

Liverpool 'Contact Agent' Over Surprise El Hadji Malick Diouf Transfer

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Liverpool are eyeing a shock move for a Premier League star, according to reports, with FSG potentially realising the mistake they may have made.

Andoni Iraola's side could be in the market for a new left-sided defender in January with Milos Kerkez struggling for form since his £40million move from Bournemouth in 2025.

Kerkez shared responsibilities with Andy Robertson for the left-back role last season, and although the Scot has since moved on to sign for Tottenham, Kostas Tsimikas has returned from his loan at Roma and came in to start the 3-2 defeat at Fenerbahce on Wednesday.

While Kerkez is still Andoni Iraola's first-choice in that position, the Reds don't appear to be convinced about the Hungarian with alternatives eyed ahead of the January transfer window.

Pundit Alan Shearer certainly isn't, claiming that he believes Robertson is better than both Kerkez and Tsimikas.

Now, according to Swiss outlet 4-4-2.ch, Liverpool are plotting a potential move for El Hadji Malick Diouf at Brentford.

Diouf for Brentford in the Premier League

A fee of between £41.7million-£50m has been discussed, though the ex-Slavia Prague man has a contract to run until 2031 and Brentford might want more.

As well as Liverpool, Chelsea are said to be in the market for Diouf, according to the same report, with Xabi Alonso searching for a like-for-like replacement for Marc Cucurella, who left to join Real Madrid in June.

The 21-year-old joined Brentford in a £40m deal from West Ham in August, but has struggled for regular game time since. Indeed, Diouf has started just one Premier League match for the Bees and that came in the 2-2 draw at Aston Villa last month. The Senegalese was an unused substitute in the 2-0 win against Nottingham Forest last time out.

He was a standout performer for West Ham last season, despite the Hammers' relegation from the Premier League. It was no surprise to see him move back to the Premier League in the summer, with Brentford picking up his services despite interest from elsewhere.

But an exit from the Gtech Community Stadium has now been mooted, with Keith Andrews preferring Keane Lewis-Potter and even Aaron Hickey at left-back, meaning Diouf is third-choice.

Should Liverpool sign Diouf, he would add pace and an extra attacking threat down the left side. His athleticism is his biggest asset, and he is more than capable of getting up and down the flank.

Diouf has a high ceiling, and his former manager Nuno Espirito Santo heaped praise on him during his time at West Ham. “He’s a beautiful player," the Portuguese boss said in November 2025.

"He’s so sensitive, and the way he arrived here - I think he’s living a dream. He has a lot of talent, but he has things to improve. He’s like a diamond you have to polish. We have great responsibilities, but we have to enjoy what we do, and I think Malick does that.”

Introduction: An Orient Express Adventure Via Africa

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Welcome to my next trip report series, covering a trip that I recently wrapped up, which will include reviews of flights and a cruise. This will be a relatively short series, since the trip didn’t last that long, and had a specific purpose. However, as usual, I tried to take an unconventional routing to get to and from the destination, so I could review new experiences.

You can expect reviews of Air Algerie and Royal Air Maroc business class, as well as a review of Orient Express Sailing Yachts, which is Orient Express’ new cruising venture. In this post I’d like to share a basic outline of the trip, and then I’ll have individual review installments in the near future.

The primary reason for this trip was simple, and it was to take a cruise with Orient Express Sailing Yachts. It’s a fascinating time for the luxury cruising industry, and in particular, the increasing number of new lines associated with existing luxury hospitality brands.

Prior to this year, I hadn’t taken a cruise with any cruise line since I was a child, so I’ve been curious to check this all out. At the beginning of the year, I reviewed Ritz-Carlton Yacht Collection, which was surprisingly lovely, and a very nice reintroduction to cruising.

Then over the summer, I reviewed Four Seasons Yachts, which was definitely upmarket, but had some growing pains.

The newest entrant into this market is Orient Express Sailing Yachts, and arguably it takes luxury cruising to a new level, so you can expect a full review of the experience. Full disclosure — Ford was invited on the sailing (so traveled as a guest of Orient Express) and I tagged along. I’ll still review the experience objectively, of course, but I’ll say upfront that Orient Express simply nailed it, and this matched the feedback Ford has received from clients who have sailed with the company.

Anyway, we sailed with Orient Express Sailing Yachts on a four night journey that started in Marseille, France, and ended in Malaga, Spain, so we needed to position to and from there.

I’m of course always looking for an interesting airline angle when I travel long haul, since my goal is always to try as many new airline products as possible. So rather than flying to Marseille and from Malaga on the “typical” airlines (Air France, British Airways, Lufthansa, etc.), I tried to see if I could instead check some airlines off my list that I’ve been wanting to try. I accomplished that, and below is what my routing looked like.

While I sent Ford direct, I booked a four segment routing for myself, spread across three different tickets.

I had heard a lot about Air Algerie over the years (and not in a good way!), so I decided I finally had to give the carrier a try. Air Algerie typically has very high business class fares, despite the carrier’s lackluster product.

However, I found one decent fare on the carrier’s only route from North America, from Montreal (YUL) to Algiers (ALG) to Tunis (TUN). Okay, that doesn’t start where I’d like to start, and it doesn’t end where I’d like to end, but that has never stopped me if the price is right. 😉

This included a flight in Air Algerie’s Airbus A330-900neo business class across the Atlantic, and then a connecting flight in Avion Express’ Airbus A320neo business class for the short hop in Africa (this was a wet leased flight operated on behalf of Air Algerie). So I booked the following in business class for $1,171 in cash:

10/03 AH2701 Montreal to Algiers departing 4:10PM arriving 5:00AM (+1 day) 10/04 AH4000 Algiers to Tunis departing 7:10AM arriving 8:30AM

From there, I of course had to get from Tunis to Marseille (MRS), so I had booked Tunisair’s A320 business class for $270, with the following schedule:

10/04 TU902 Tunis to Marseille departing 1:05PM arriving 3:40PM

Unfortunately I ended up being downgraded shortly before departure due to an aircraft swap. That’s because the flight was swapped to a wet leased Fly2Sky Airbus A320, and I guess they don’t offer business class on those planes. I’m still waiting on any sort of a refund.

Suffice it to say that the outbound wasn’t that smooth, between Air Algerie just royally sucking, and Tunisair downgrading me. I’m happy I had the experience, though!

I should also mention that I had to position to Montreal, so I did that in Air Canada Rouge economy, and flew the following for 11,500 avianca lifemiles plus $54 in taxes & fees:

10/04 AC1651 Miami to Montreal departing 7:55AM arriving 11:28AM

The return portion of our trip was a little more straightforward. Our priority was to get home the same day as our cruise arrived, since we wanted to minimize time away from our kids, and had a family event the next day. So we booked the one routing that would best get us home the same day, which was on Royal Air Maroc, from Malaga (AGP) to Casablanca (CMN) to Miami (MIA), with the first flight in Royal Air Maroc’s Boeing 737-800 business class, and the second flight in Royal Air Maroc’s Boeing 787-8 business class.

I booked one ticket using 57,500 American AAdvantage miles plus $85.72 in taxes & fees, while the other ticket was booked with cash, at the cost of $1,620, with the following itinerary:

10/09 AT989 Malaga to Casablanca departing 2:15PM arriving 1:30PM 10/09 AT204 Casablanca to Miami departing 3:00PM arriving 7:45PM

Obviously paying cash for a ticket wasn’t ideal, but that still ended up being less out of pocket than flying British Airways business class through London on awards, factoring in the carrier surcharges.

On this trip, we just had a single hotel night ahead of our cruise in Marseille (in order to build in a buffer), and we decided to stay at the Hôtel Dieu Intercontinental Marseille. I booked this using an IHG free night award, and it cost 60,000 points before applying the award, so I just had to redeem the difference in points.

I had intended to review the hotel, but I have to apologize, it just didn’t end up working out (since I pride myself in thoroughly reviewing hotels). To be honest, I was dead tired from the journey over, and then we had some drama to deal with back at home most of the afternoon. Then the next morning I prioritized sleeping in, as I was just getting over a cold, rather than getting up at 5AM so I could photograph all parts of the hotel.

So I’m sorry about that. My conclusion is that the hotel has a central location, and from the outside you’d think it’s super charming, but the interior feels mismatched and rather uninspiring and generic.

We had an absolutely amazing time cruising with Orient Express Sailing Yachts, and I was blown away by the experience. What I was less impressed by was my flights to and from Europe, which I routed via Africa in both directions. Air Algerie was downright terrible, while Royal Air Maroc was just unnecessarily bad.

RLUSD Is Not Coming for XRP: Garlinghouse Clarifies Ripple’s Bigger XRPL Vision as Trader Spots Setup Behind 71% Price Explosion

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ripple CEO Bradley Garlinghouse is pushing back against the idea that the company’s growing RLUSD stablecoin ambitions come at XRP’s expense, insisting the stablecoin is targeting a different market altogether.

As Ripple sharpens its vision for the XRP Ledger, a separate development is drawing attention to XRP’s price prospects, with prominent crypto trader Ali Charts spotting a chart pattern that previously preceded a staggering 71% rally.

Garlinghouse sees the RLUSD stablecoin as a way to bring more activity and practical use cases to the XRP Ledger, not as a competitor to XRP. Instead of treating the stablecoin as a rival, he framed its growth as part of the broader momentum behind XRP, arguing that both assets can play complementary roles in expanding the ecosystem.

“RLUSD isn’t competing with XRP. It’s competing with other stablecoins. Use case matters, and I want those use cases running on the XRPL,” Garlinghouse wrote on X.

By targeting the wider market, Ripple hopes to expand the range of financial applications operating on its blockchain. Garlinghouse emphasized that the company wants RLUSD-powered use cases to run on XRPL, potentially attracting more businesses, developers, and institutional users to the network.

Garlinghouse was responding to XRP community researcher WrathofKahneman, who argued that RLUSD’s expansion could ultimately benefit XRP by increasing liquidity across the XRP Ledger. “RLUSD is great for XRP. More XRPL liquidity makes XRP easier to use in the long run & it isn’t replacing XRP where it wasn’t used,” the researcher wrote.

However, WrathofKahneman cautioned that the benefits may not immediately translate into significant price gains without direct demand for XRP. “The problem is that w/out direct use, price impact is 2nd order,” he added.

The researcher also raised a broader question about XRP’s future role in the ecosystem: “Will XRP become a specialized institutional asset with gated liquidity, or be ubiquitous?”

Agreed! RLUSD isn’t competing with XRP. It’s competing with other stablecoins. Use case matters, and I want those use cases running on the XRPL.

The debate is particularly relevant as XRP trades around $1.28, well below its previous all-time high of $3.65, even as Ripple continues to expand its institutional footprint. While growing adoption could strengthen the XRP Ledger, investors are still watching to see whether that progress will translate into stronger demand and sustained price appreciation for XRP.

Meanwhile, crypto analyst Ali Charts has identified a potentially bullish technical signal on XRP’s four-hour chart, suggesting the token could be gearing up for another significant move. According to the analyst, XRP recently recorded its tightest Bollinger Band squeeze since August before breaking above the upper band, which could signal building upward momentum.

The setup is drawing attention because a similar period of extreme volatility compression preceded an impressive rally earlier this year. Ali Charts noted that the last time XRP’s Bollinger Bands were this tightly compressed, the token surged nearly 71% in just three days.

The pundit believes another parabolic price move could now be taking shape. At its current price of around $1.16, a repeat of the previous 71% surge would push XRP to approximately $2.19. Such a move would represent a substantial recovery for the token, potentially bringing it closer to key resistance levels and reigniting bullish sentiment across the XRP market.

Traders will be watching whether XRP can sustain its move above the upper Bollinger Band and attract enough buying pressure to confirm a breakout. However, a failure to hold the gains could turn the latest bullish signal into another false start.

This Off-Grid ‘Treehouse’ Is Perched 60 Feet in the Air—and Built From Shipping Containers

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you've ever dreamed of sleeping among the trees, a property in the Epworth, GA, forest might be just the place.

"The structure itself is very unique. It's a steel watchtower built to commercial grade but for residential purposes, purely for leisurely enjoyment, for getaways, for the adventurer at heart," says listing agent Logan Fitts with Mountain Sotheby's International Realty.

Listed for $1.2 million, the 60-foot-high tower sits on 14.31 acres of land adjacent to the Chattahoochee National Forest with about 3,000 feet of river frontage.

"I was definitely awestruck first by the property itself, the river frontage, the waterfalls on the property, all of the hemlock trees dispersed throughout the property," Fitts says. "But then when we went to the top of the property where the watchtower was, I was in total awe."

Two shipping containers are perched atop the tower, creating the living space.

"The main structure is the bedroom efficiency with a bed, sitting area, stove, kitchenette. There's a landing between and then across is the bathroom area with a full shower, toilet, and double vanity," he explains. "Then there's a spiral staircase to the rooftop fire pit and lounge area."

And while there is a lift for things like suitcases and packages, people need to walk up 82 steel-grated stairs, which Fitts admits can be a bit daunting.

"I was actually nervous going up the tower for the first time because it's high and it is an experience. But once I did it, I said, 'This is what real mountain experiences are like. This is something that people remember for the rest of their lives.'"

"You are surrounded by just foliage, so when you're at the top, you just see a full panoramic view of the mountains and valley across one way, and you hear the roaring river on the property down below. You can actually see the waterfall," Fitts says.

"It's like a glass birdhouse at the top of this mountain, and you feel like you're definitely elevated and away from everything, but still connected to nature."

To get to the property itself requires a four-wheel-drive vehicle, because some of the roads are gravel and visitors need to cross the Jacks River, literally.

"Anyone with a four-wheel-drive or some type of ground clearance should have zero issue forging the river," Fitts explains, adding that the river is shallow at the access point. "It's not scary if you've done it before, and once you do it, it's like 'OK, now I'm an adventurer.'"

The current owner has had the place for about a decade and completed the tower house in 2023. Since then, it has been a short-term rental, bringing in about $92,000 annually during a nine-month rental period.

The owner would "love to pass it to someone that would take it, create a new legacy, and maybe make it even better," Fitts says.

"I think more than anything, he wants a true outdoors lover to really appreciate this property and not just see a metal dwelling in the middle of the forest. He built it for people to really enjoy nature, embrace nature, and take care of nature."

While the property itself is off-grid with solar power, a well, septic system, and generator, it’s not physically far from civilization.

"Probably the property is maybe 30 minutes from downtown Blue Ridge," Fitts says, adding it just feels remote. "Once you're there, you're in a totally different world. Your neighbors are trees and wildlife."

Tiffani Sherman is an experienced freelance writer based in the Tampa, Florida area who covers real estate, personal finance, travel, and more for a variety of print and online publications. She works to support her travel habit and has been to all seven continents and about 100 countries. Tiffani is a proud Duke Blue Devil.

‘Full House’ Star Jodie Sweetin Says She Had ‘$190 in My Bank Account’ Before Filming ‘Fuller House’: ‘I Had to Borrow Money the First Week’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Jodie Sweetin, who first shot to fame as Stephanie Tanner on the beloved sitcom “Full House,” is opening up about her financial struggles over the years, particularly how close she came to running out of funds before returning to the show’s 2016 reboot, “Fuller House.”

“I had to borrow money the first week I was working on ‘Fuller House,’ and I [skittered] into my first paycheck with like $190 in my bank account,” Sweetin revealed on a recent episode of the “We’re Still Here, Babe” podcast. “And nobody that I worked with knew that.” Related Stories Bradley Cooper and Danny McBride's 'G.I. Joe' Movie Set for 2028; Paramount Shifts New 'Paranormal Activity' to Halloween 2027 'Jimmy' Review: Cornball James Stewart Biopic Gets the Manner, but Not the Man

Sweetin has been open about her struggles with addiction, and also discussed how those habits left her with “no money” at a certain point, leading her to broaden her employment search to non-Hollywood gigs. “I didn’t have [an] ego about it,” Sweetin said. “I didn’t care that I was, quote, unquote, working a normal job. I think other people again were like, ‘What are you doing here?’ And I was like, ‘Paying my bills. Kids don’t pay for themselves.’”

She continued: “And not like I was ever a materialistic person, but you learn humility when you are at Target, and you’ve got to turn around and, you know, put some shit back, because you don’t have the money on your debit card.”

“Full House” ran for eight seasons, from 1987 to 1995. “Fuller House” ran for five seasons on Netflix, totalling nearly 80 episodes. Dropout’s Vic Michaelis Wants to Toss Their Star Wars Character Out a Window

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Michaelis' Runa is the breakout star of Zero Company

Dropout star Vic Michaelis used to fall asleep to Attack of the Clones. Not because they hated it, like so many Star Wars prequel detractors, but because it was their comfort watch. “I've seen the first 45 minutes of Episode II, I want to say, 300 times,” Michaelis told Polygon. “It's so comforting. The prequels hold a place in my heart where it is like nothing bad can happen when I'm watching them.”

Michaelis has now gone from watching Star Wars to being a part of the larger Star Wars universe. In Star Wars Zero Company, they played breakout character Runa Blask, the wise-cracking advisor to the titular mercenary group, led by Captain Hawks.

Michaelis hails from the improv comedy world, but they’ve done film and scripted TV shows as well. Zero Company was their first-ever video game role. “I was so simultaneously terrified, obviously, of the video game aspect of it and also, you know, the Star Wars of it all.”

Turns out, that fear was unnecessary. The team at Bit Reactor was familiar with Michaelis’ Dropout work, and that was part of the reason they wanted Michaelis for the role.

Their improv background helped influence their performance, though they weren’t changing lines or jokes. “The improv is so much about the preparation in a way that I think people don't really know.” Instead, all of Runa’s best quips were already in the very strong script. “All of my favorite lines that Runa had were written or pitched lines from the writers,” Michaelis said.

Among those lines were many instances of Runa saying “wizard,” which basically means “cool” in Star Wars. “Runa's very tall, everything is ‘wizard,’” Michaelis said with a laugh, joking they must have said the word “500 times.”

Fans have glommed onto Runa for her constant jokes and ribbing of Hawks. “I'm seeing a lot of people being like, ‘Runa, I love her, and I wanna throw her out a window.’ Which I really resonate with, and I think was part of what we were going for,” Michaelis said. “Big sister vibes.”

Runa can’t be thrown out a window. She actually can’t be harmed at all. While the game features a permadeath feature for its operators, Runa doesn’t go on combat missions and doesn’t leave The Den, Zero Company’s headquarters.

“I did start seeing people devastated and heartbroken that some characters that they put a lot of time and energy into didn't make it through,” Michaelis said. “Do I wish people sort of mourned me in that way? Maybe. I don't know. I’m going to be thinking about that.”

Before Runa was a member of Zero Company, she was a self-described “freelance consultant to the top corporations and families in the galaxy.” AKA, a Separatist financial fixer. She desires a fortune that working for Zero Company will never grant her. As she tells Hawks, if she had that fortune, she’d want to buy a moon and run a resort. Michaelis has much humbler aspirations if they were to live in the world of Star Wars. “I would want to be a small business owner,” Michaelis said, and started describing what would make for a very fun Disney Plus show.

“I want to do a comedy night at my bookstore and all of a sudden, you've got a bunch of interplanetary politics or some red tape of why I can't do this. Then I'm marching up to the Republic with my civil city ordinances, trying to get this comedy night happening, and I'm accidentally now involved and implicated in a sort of a greater plot,” Michaelis said. “And all I wanted to do was try and get a couple of standups at my bookstore at night so I could sell wine after hours.”

If Star Trek can have a critically acclaimed animated sitcom, why can’t Star Wars have a series about a store owner just trying to get a comedy night off the ground? Your move, Disney.
